```
export const meta = {
  name: 'review-scorer-changes',
  description: 'Review the ad-scorer changes across 5 dimensions, then adversarially verify each finding',
  phases: [
    { title: 'Review', detail: 'one agent per review dimension' },
    { title: 'Verify', detail: 'two refuters per finding, majority kills it' },
  ],
}

const CONTEXT = `
Repo: /Users/adityakadiyan/Developer/minimalist-ad-tool — a PM-assignment tool that scores skincare ad copy
against a rulebook for the Indian brand Minimalist. Read CLAUDE.md first; its rules are binding
(no invented facts/citations/rules, prompts live in /prompts as plain files, model never assigns severity,
scope is deliberately narrow — do not propose new features).

Files that just changed (read them all before judging):
- web/src/app/api/score/route.ts   (POST /api/score; rule 1 in code, rules 2a/2b/3/4/5 via claude-sonnet-5)
- web/src/app/scorer.tsx           (client UI: textarea, product dropdown, results, inline span highlighting)
- web/src/app/page.tsx             (server component, lists product handles)
- prompts/rules.md                 (THE rulebook — severity and buckets come from here)
- prompts/scorer.md                (the model prompt template)
- evidence/products/*.json         (8 Shopify product JSONs; the 'title' field carries the concentration)

What changed in this round:
1. Rule 1 now checks every active it finds against whichever of the 8 product titles owns that active,
   not only the product selected in the dropdown. The dropdown is an override for actives the selected
   product owns. An active no title accounts for is a REVIEW with note "no source to verify against".
   Name matching picks the NEAREST name to the percentage, with longest-name as a tie-break.
2. prompts/rules.md gained: six-rule-ID wording, a buckets line, and an "Implementation notes" section.
3. UI: inline highlighting of flagged spans over the scored ad text, all three buckets always shown
   (with "nothing fired" when empty), and a "N rules checked, M fired." line under the verdict.

Report only defects that are real in THIS code. No style preferences, no speculative hardening,
no feature suggestions. For each finding give a concrete failing input and the wrong output it produces.
`

const FINDINGS = {
  type: 'object',
  properties: {
    findings: {
      type: 'array',
      items: {
        type: 'object',
        properties: {
          title: { type: 'string' },
          file: { type: 'string' },
          line: { type: 'number' },
          failing_input: { type: 'string' },
          wrong_output: { type: 'string' },
          why_it_matters: { type: 'string' },
        },
        required: ['title', 'file', 'failing_input', 'wrong_output', 'why_it_matters'],
      },
    },
  },
  required: ['findings'],
}

const VERDICT = {
  type: 'object',
  properties: {
    refuted: { type: 'boolean' },
    reasoning: { type: 'string' },
  },
  required: ['refuted', 'reasoning'],
}

const DIMENSIONS = [
  {
    key: 'rule1',
    prompt: `${CONTEXT}

Your dimension: the rule-1 concentration checker in route.ts (activeFromTitle, tokenize, normalize,
findPercentages, mapToChars, nameNearPercent, scoreRule1).

Trace it by hand against the 8 real product titles. Hunt specifically for:
- percentages that get attributed to the wrong active (nearest-match logic, tie-breaks, window edges)
- concentrations that SHOULD block but silently pass, or correct ones that falsely block
- normalize()/mapToChars() offset arithmetic — can the returned span be wrong or out of bounds?
- the owners Map and the selected-product override — can the override pick the wrong source?
- "02%" vs "2%", "0.1%", "10 %", percentages inside other numbers, repeated actives in one ad
Write out the actual ad strings you tested and what the code returns for each.`,
  },
  {
    key: 'route',
    prompt: `${CONTEXT}

Your dimension: the route contract in route.ts outside rule 1 — request parsing, product_handle
validation and path handling, loadActives/loadSourceFacts, the model call, parseModelJson,
severityForRule4, verdictFor, the bucket filters, and the JSON response shape.

Hunt for: findings silently dropped, severity coming from the model rather than the rulebook,
a verdict that disagrees with the findings it returned, unhandled model output shapes, rule IDs
that fall through the filters, and any way the API key or a file path could go somewhere it shouldn't.`,
  },
  {
    key: 'ui',
    prompt: `${CONTEXT}

Your dimension: web/src/app/scorer.tsx and page.tsx.

Focus hard on highlightRanges/MarkedUpAd: overlapping spans, adjacent spans, a span that repeats in
the text, a span not present in the text, a span at index 0 or at the very end, and whether the merge
loop can drop text or duplicate it. Reconstruct the rendered string from the algorithm and check it
equals the original ad text with markers inserted — character for character.
Also: the "N rules checked, M fired" count, the empty-bucket rendering, and whether the highlighted
text can drift out of sync with what was actually scored.`,
  },
  {
    key: 'rulebook',
    prompt: `${CONTEXT}

Your dimension: consistency between prompts/rules.md, prompts/scorer.md and route.ts.

Check that: every rule ID in rules.md has a severity the route can resolve and vice versa; the buckets
line in rules.md matches the BUCKETS object in the route; the Implementation notes describe what the
code ACTUALLY does (they claim a five-word window, nearest-match, an override, REVIEW for an active in
no title, and thinking off — verify each against the code); the scorer prompt's placeholders match what
the route substitutes; and the prompt cannot make the model assign severity.
Flag any statement in rules.md or scorer.md that is now false about the code. Quote the exact line.`,
  },

the client bundle. Quote the exact offending line. If the code is clean on a point, say so and move on.`,
  },
]

phase('Review')
const results = await pipeline(
  DIMENSIONS,
  (d) => agent(d.prompt, { label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS }),
  (review, d) => {
    const found = (review && review.findings) || []
    if (!found.length) return []
    return parallel(
      found.map((f) => () =>
        parallel([
          () =>
            agent(
              `${CONTEXT}\n\nA reviewer claims this defect:\n\nTITLE: ${f.title}\nFILE: ${f.file}\nFAILING INPUT: ${f.failing_input}\nCLAIMED WRONG OUTPUT: ${f.wrong_output}\n\nRead the actual code and try to REFUTE it. Run the failing input through the code by hand, line by line. If the code already handles this, or the claimed output is not what the code produces, or the "defect" is a style preference rather than a wrong result, refute it. Default to refuted=true when uncertain.`,
              { label: `refute:${d.key}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT },
            ),
          () =>
            agent(
              `${CONTEXT}\n\nA reviewer claims this defect:\n\nTITLE: ${f.title}\nFILE: ${f.file}\nFAILING INPUT: ${f.failing_input}\nCLAIMED WRONG OUTPUT: ${f.wrong_output}\n\nDoes this matter for THIS tool? It scores ad copy for a PM assignment; the evaluators care about the rulebook being enforced correctly, not about hardening against hostile input. Refute the finding if it is unreachable in practice, cosmetic, or would require the marketer to do something no marketer would do. Confirm it only if a plausible ad would produce a wrong verdict, a wrong severity, a wrong span, or a false claim in the rulebook. Default to refuted=true when uncertain.`,
              { label: `impact:${d.key}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT },
            ),
        ]).then((votes) => {
          const live = votes.filter(Boolean)
          const kills = live.filter((v) => v.refuted).length
          return kills === 0 && live.length === 2 ? { ...f, dimension: d.key } : null
        }),
      ),
    )
  },
)

const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean)
log(`${confirmed.length} finding(s) survived both refuters`)
return { confirmed }

```

**description**: `Review diff scope while workflow runs`

**Result**

```
 prompts/rules.md               |  31 +++++++-
 web/src/app/api/score/route.ts | 165 ++++++++++++++++++++++++++++-------------
 web/src/app/scorer.tsx         | 119 +++++++++++++++++++++++------
 3 files changed, 239 insertions(+), 76 deletions(-)
=== rules.md tail ===

## Implementation notes

Decisions the scorer had to make that this file didn't settle. Recorded here so
the next person doesn't have to read the route to find them.

**Adjacency for rule 1 is five words.** "A percentage next to an active" needed a
number. Five words either side of the percentage catches "Salicylic Acid + LHA
0.2%" and "It's still exactly 10% Vitamin B5" while ignoring a percentage a
dozen words away from the nearest active name. Where several actives match
inside that window, the longest name wins, so "Salicylic Acid + LHA" is checked
against the cleanser rather than against the Salicylic Acid serum.

**An active in no product title is REVIEW, not BLOCK.** Rule 1 checks every
active it finds against whichever of the product JSONs owns that active, not
only against the product picked in the dropdown — a routine ad names three
products and only one can be selected. The dropdown is an override: when the
selected product owns the active, its title wins. An active that no title
accounts for gets REVIEW with "no source to verify against", because there is
nothing to check the number against and silence would read as approval.

**Thinking is off on the model call.** Output is capped at 1500 tokens and
thinking tokens count against that cap, so leaving it on can truncate the JSON
mid-object. If the model needs room to reason about a rule later, the cap moves
first.
```

```
p='web/src/app/api/score/route.ts'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

# (4) normalize replaced characters instead of deleting them: "Alpha-Arbutin" -> "alpha arbutin".
# 1:1 replacement also keeps normalized length equal to source length, so the offset maths is exact.
s = s.replace('''function normalize(s: string): string {
  return s.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9+%. ]/g, "");
}''',
'''// Replaces, never deletes: a 1:1 swap keeps the normalized length equal to the
// source length, so offsets map straight back. Deleting made "Alpha-Arbutin"
// into "alphaarbutin", which no active name could ever match.
function normalize(s: string): string {
  return s.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9+%.]/g, " ");
}''')

# (1)(2)(3) nearest-with-ties -> adjacency required, name-before-number wins
old_start = s.index('// Looks for an active\'s name within WINDOW_WORDS either side of a percentage.')
old_end = s.index('function scoreRule1(')
new = '''// Looks for the active whose concentration a percentage is stating.
//
// The name has to be immediately adjacent to the number — nothing but spaces and
// punctuation between them. "Niacinamide 10%" states a concentration; "Flat 20%
// off Niacinamide" and "Vitamin C 10%: 92% of users" do not, and neither does the
// "100% vegan" that rules.md itself calls legitimate copy.
//
// When names sit on both sides, the one BEFORE the number wins: that is the form
// all eight product titles use, and the form the brand's copy uses. Without it,
// "Retinal 10% Niacinamide 10%" hands the 10% to Niacinamide, whose page says
// 10%, and a 100x overstatement of a retinoid passes. Distance, then name length,
// break what the first test leaves tied — which is what sends "Salicylic Acid +
// LHA 0.2%" to the cleanser rather than to bare LHA.
function nameNearPercent(
  text: string,
  tokens: Token[],
  hit: PercentHit,
  names: string[],
): { name: string; start: number; end: number } | null {
  const hitIndex = tokens.findIndex((t) => t.end > hit.start && t.start < hit.end);
  if (hitIndex === -1) return null;

  const from = Math.max(0, hitIndex - WINDOW_WORDS);
  const to = Math.min(tokens.length - 1, hitIndex + WINDOW_WORDS);
  const window = tokens.slice(from, to + 1);
  const windowText = normalize(window.map((t) => t.text).join(" "));

  type Candidate = { name: string; start: number; end: number; precedes: boolean; distance: number };
  let best: Candidate | null = null;

  for (const name of names) {
    const needle = normalize(name);
    if (needle.trim().length === 0) continue;

    // Every occurrence, not just the first — the same active can appear twice
    // in one window with only one of them next to this percentage.
    for (let at = windowText.indexOf(needle); at !== -1; at = windowText.indexOf(needle, at + 1)) {
      const chars = mapToChars(window, at, needle.length);
      if (chars === null) continue;

      const precedes = chars.end <= hit.start;
      const follows = hit.end <= chars.start;
      if (!precedes && !follows) continue; // overlapping the number itself

      const between = precedes
        ? text.slice(chars.end, hit.start)
        : text.slice(hit.end, chars.start);
      if (/[a-z0-9]/i.test(between)) continue; // a word sits in between: not a concentration

      const candidate: Candidate = {
        name,
        start: Math.min(chars.start, hit.start),
        end: Math.max(chars.end, hit.end),
        precedes,
        distance: between.length,
      };

      if (best === null || betterMatch(candidate, best)) best = candidate;
    }
  }

  return best === null ? null : { name: best.name, start: best.start, end: best.end };
}

function betterMatch(
  a: { precedes: boolean; distance: number; name: string },
  b: { precedes: boolean; distance: number; name: string },
): boolean {
  if (a.precedes !== b.precedes) return a.precedes;
  if (a.distance !== b.distance) return a.distance < b.distance;
  return normalize(a.name).trim().length > normalize(b.name).trim().length;
}

'''
s = s[:old_start] + new + s[old_end:]

# (6) placeholders: replace every occurrence, not just the first
s = s.replace('''  const prompt = template
    .replace("{{RULES}}", rules)
    .replace("{{SOURCE_FACTS}}", sourceFacts)
    .replace("{{AD_TEXT}}", adText);''',
'''  // replaceAll, not replace: a single-occurrence replace filled whichever
  // mention came first in the file, which left the real sections holding
  // literal {{...}} markers.
  const prompt = template
    .replaceAll("{{RULES}}", rules)
    .replaceAll("{{SOURCE_FACTS}}", sourceFacts)
    .replaceAll("{{AD_TEXT}}", adText);''')

open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
print("route patched")
```

```
# highlight cursor: key by rule id AND span, so two rules quoting the same words
# both land on it and merge, while one rule firing twice advances.
p2='web/src/app/scorer.tsx'
t=open(p2,encoding='utf-8').read()
old2 = '''  // Two findings can quote the same words — rule 1 fires once per occurrence of
  // a wrong concentration. Give each one the next unclaimed occurrence so the
  // second is marked in the second place instead of on top of the first.
  const searchFrom = new Map<string, number>();

  for (const finding of findings) {
    if (!finding.span) continue;
    const from = searchFrom.get(finding.span) ?? 0;
    const start = adText.indexOf(finding.span, from);
    if (start === -1) continue; // model returned a span that isn't in the text
    searchFrom.set(finding.span, start + finding.span.length);
    ranges.push({ start, end: start + finding.span.length, ids: [finding.id] });
  }'''
new2 = '''  // Keyed by rule AND span, not span alone. One rule firing twice on the same
  // words means two places in the ad, so the cursor advances. Two rules quoting
  // the same headline means one place with two rule numbers on it, so each rule
  // starts its own search and the merge below joins them.
  const searchFrom = new Map<string, number>();

  for (const finding of findings) {
    if (!finding.span) continue;
    const key = `${finding.id}\\u0000${finding.span}`;
    const from = searchFrom.get(key) ?? 0;
    const start = adText.indexOf(finding.span, from);
    if (start === -1) continue; // model returned a span that isn't in the text
    searchFrom.set(key, start + finding.span.length);
    ranges.push({ start, end: start + finding.span.length, ids: [finding.id] });
  }'''
assert old2 in t
open(p2,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(t.replace(old2,new2))
print("ok")
```

```
ok
## Implementation notes

Decisions the scorer had to make that this file didn't settle. Recorded here so
the next person doesn't have to read the route to find them.

**Rule 1 only reads a percentage that is touching an active's name.** "A
percentage next to an active" needed a definition. The test is that nothing but
spaces and punctuation sits between the name and the number: "Niacinamide 10%",
"10% Vitamin B5" and "Retinal (0.1%)" are concentration claims; "Flat 20% off
Niacinamide", "Vitamin C 10%: 92% of users" and the "100% vegan" this file calls
legitimate under rule 3 are not, and rule 1 leaves all three alone.

Where names sit on both sides of a number, the one BEFORE it wins — the form
every product title uses. Without that, "Retinal 10% Niacinamide 10%" hands the
10% to Niacinamide, whose page says 10%, and a hundredfold overstatement of a
retinoid reads as clean. Distance and then name length break what is still tied,
which is what sends "Salicylic Acid + LHA 0.2%" to the cleanser and not to bare
LHA.

**Every active is checked against whichever title owns it, not just the selected
one.** A routine ad names three products and only one can be picked in the
dropdown, so checking against the selection alone made the other two products'
correct concentrations look unverifiable. The dropdown is now an override: when
the selected product owns the active, its title wins over any other title
naming the same active.

**Known gap: rule 1 only recognises the actives named in the eight titles.** The
recognised names are parsed out of those titles — Alpha Arbutin, Niacinamide,
Retinal, Salicylic Acid, LHA, Vitamin B5, Vitamin C. A percentage attached to an
ingredient none of them names, "Retinol 1%" or "Hyaluronic Acid 2%", is not
checked and produces nothing. The route still carries the REVIEW with "no source
to verify against" for an active no title accounts for, but no input can reach it
today. Closing this needs a decision about what counts as an ingredient name when
the evidence doesn't have one, and guessing would flag "Flat 20% off" as an
active. Open, not solved.

**Thinking is off on the model call.** Output is capped at 1500 tokens and
thinking tokens count against that cap, so leaving it on can truncate the JSON
mid-object. If the model needs room to reason about a rule later, the cap moves
first.
```

## Assistant — 15:23:25

Committed. All three changes are in, plus fixes for defects an adversarial review turned up.

**1. Rule 1 across all 8 products.** Each active resolves to whichever title owns it; the dropdown overrides for actives the selected product owns. Your routine ad now returns one BLOCK on the wrong `0.2%` and nothing on the two correct products — previously two REVIEWs of noise.

**2. `rules.md`** — six rule IDs, the buckets line, and an Implementation notes section.

**3. UX** — inline highlights with rule numbers over the scored text, all three sections always shown, "N rules checked, M fired" under the verdict.

**Bugs found and fixed along the way.** I ran a 45-agent adversarial review over the diff; nine findings survived two refuters each. Five were real code defects, all mine from this round:

| Defect | Effect |
|---|---|
| Ties resolved by longest name | `Retinal 10% Niacinamide 10%` → the 10% went to Niacinamide, whose page says 10%. A 100× retinoid overstatement **passed** |
| Same tie, other direction | `Retinal 0.1% Niacinamide 10%` — both correct — **false BLOCK**, and the dropdown couldn't override it |
| Any % within 5 words counted | `Flat 20% off Niacinamide 10%` and `100% vegan` **hard-blocked**, with fixes reading "Flat 10% off", "10% vegan" |
| `normalize()` deleted hyphens | `Alpha-Arbutin 5%` never matched — wrong concentration, **silent pass** |
| Highlight keyed by span alone | Two rules quoting one headline → second rule's marker **dropped** |

Fixes: the name must be *touching* the number (only spaces/punctuation between), a name *before* the number wins, punctuation is replaced not deleted, and highlights key on rule+span. 20 regression cases pass, including every input the review broke.

**One more, worth your attention:** `prompts/scorer.md` named its own placeholders in the header prose, and I was substituting only the first occurrence — so the rulebook was being pasted into a sentence at the top while `## The rulebook`, `## Source facts` and `## Ad text` kept literal `{{markers}}`. The model coped, which is why my earlier tests looked fine. Fixed both ends.

**Two things I changed in `rules.md` beyond what you asked, because they'd become false:**

- "PASS means none of these rules fired" — your verdict ladder returns PASS when only FLAG/INFO fired. Reworded to "nothing fired that gates spend."
- The "no source to verify against" REVIEW you specified **can't currently fire.** Rule 1 only recognises actives named in the 8 titles, and every one of those has a percentage, so `Retinol 1%` produces silence rather than a REVIEW. I kept the branch and wrote it up as an open gap rather than build an unknown-ingredient detector — any heuristic loose enough to catch "Retinol" also catches "Flat 20% off". That one needs your call.
